Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an observable acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
fully conserves an additive quantity
N={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. Let
\|\epsilon(x)\|
be the error in measuring the effects of the target observable
\mathsf{E}
, as defined in Eq. (
7
). Then for all
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(37)

where
{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)
denotes the quantum Fisher information of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
in the state
\xi
. Additionally, if
\mathsf{E}
is an extremal observable and
{\mathcal{M}}
is a measurement scheme for
\mathsf{E}
, then for all
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\|\leqslant{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(38)

Proof.

Let
\{q_{i},\phi_{i}\}
be an arbitrary ensemble of unit vectors that satisfies
\xi=\sum_{i}q_{i}P_{\phi_{i}}
. We may thus write
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)=\sum_{i}q_{i}\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
, where
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)\equiv\Gamma_{P_{\phi_{i}}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
. Given the additivity of
N
and the conservation law, we may rewrite Eq. (
36
) as

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])=[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}},\epsilon(x)]+\sum_{i}q_{i}\bigg([\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)),\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\bigg).

By the sesquilinear mappings
\langle\langle A|B\rangle\rangle_{i}:=\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*}B)-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*})\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)
, Corollary
A.2
and Lemma
3.1
, we obtain the bounds

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+2\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since both
\mathsf{Z}(x)
and
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))
are effects, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant 1/2.

We thus arrive at the bound

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+\left(\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the second line follows from the concavity of the square root. By choosing the ensemble
\{q_{i},\phi_{i}\}
that gives the quantum Fisher information as in Eq. (
13
), we arrive at the bound in Eq. (
37
).

Now assume that
\mathsf{E}
is an extremal observable
[
94
]
. This implies that for any pair of observables
\mathsf{E}^{(1)}
and
\mathsf{E}^{(2)}
, and any
\lambda\in(0,1)
, the effects of
\mathsf{E}
can be decomposed as
\mathsf{E}(x)=\lambda\,\mathsf{E}^{(1)}(x)+(1-\lambda)\,\mathsf{E}^{(2)}(x)
only if
\mathsf{E}=\mathsf{E}^{(1)}=\mathsf{E}^{(2)}
. It follows that if
{\mathcal{M}}
is a measurement scheme for
\mathsf{E}
, that is, if
\epsilon=0
, then
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))=\mathsf{E}(x)
for all
i
. Consequently, we obtain the bounds
\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}
for all
i
, which gives

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\Lambda^{*}([\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\left(\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Once again choosing the ensemble that gives the quantum Fisher information, we arrive at Eq. (
38
).

∎

Appendix H
Bounds for measurement disturbance under conservation laws

Here we provide quantitative trade-off relations for measurement disturbance under additive conservation laws, both average and full. Note that here, the observable that may or may not be disturbed is not necessarily the same observable that is measured by the instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
.

Theorem H.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. Let
\|\delta(y)\|
be the disturbance of the effects of an observable
\mathsf{F}:=\{\mathsf{F}(y):y\in{\mathcal{Y}}\}
caused by
{\mathcal{I}}
, as defined in Eq. (
8
). Then for all
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+2\|\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}},

(39)

where
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}
is the channel defined in Eq. (
3
). If
\mathsf{F}
is non-disturbed by
{\mathcal{I}}
, that is, if
\delta=0
, then for all
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(40)

Proof.

By Eq. (
8
), we may write

[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}},\delta(y)]+[{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)),N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]).

Since
N
is additive, average conservation of
N
by
{\mathcal{E}}
implies that
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)=\Gamma_{\xi}(N)={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. It follows that for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
,
[A,N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]=[A,\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]
. We may therefore write
[{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)),N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]=[\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}),\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]
. Additionally, by additivity of
N
we may write
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},N])=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{F}(y),N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})={\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])
. We thus arrive at

\displaystyle[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}},\delta(y)]+[\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}),\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},N]).

(41)

By the sesquilinear mapping
\langle\langle A|B\rangle\rangle:=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*}B)-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*})\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)
and Corollary
A.2
we obtain from Eq. (
41
) the bound

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant\|[N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}},\delta(y)]\|+2\|\langle\langle N|N\rangle\rangle\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle\langle\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}|\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}\rangle\rangle\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(42)

By definition, we have
\langle\langle\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}|\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}\rangle\rangle={\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}
and
\langle\langle N|N\rangle\rangle=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)^{2}
. Noting that
\|[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}},\delta(y)]\|\leqslant 2\|N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|
, we thus obtain from Eq. (
42
) the bound given in Eq. (
39
). If
\mathsf{F}
is non-disturbed by
{\mathcal{I}}
, then
\|\delta(y)\|=0
and
{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}=\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}
for all
y
. We thus arrive at Eq. (
40
).
∎

Corollary H.1
.

Consider the set-up of Theorem
H.1
. For all
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
it also holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+2\|\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(2\|\delta(y)\|+\|\mathsf{F}(y)-\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\|\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{F}(y)
are effects and
{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}
is a channel, then by Lemma
A.2
we have
\|{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}\|\leqslant 2\|\delta(y)\|+\|\mathsf{F}(y)-\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\|
. The claim immediately follows from Eq. (
39
).
∎

Corollary H.2
.

If
\mathsf{E}
commutes with
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and is measured by the Lüders instrument
{\mathcal{I}}^{L}
, then Theorem
H.1
does not rule out non-disturbance of any observable
\mathsf{F}
that commutes with
\mathsf{E}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
always holds, then
[\mathsf{E},\mathsf{F}]=\mathds{O}
implies that
\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
. But
[\mathsf{E},{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
implies that
{{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{k}})={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{k}}
for
k=1,2
. By the multiplicability theorem (Corollary
A.1
), it holds that
{{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}A)={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}{{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}(A)
and
{{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}(A{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})={{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}(A){N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, and so we obtain
{{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]
. In such a case, the lower bound of Eq. (
40
) vanishes, and so no constraints are imposed on non-disturbance for
\mathsf{F}
.
∎

Proposition H.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
fully conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. Let
\|\delta(y)\|
be the disturbance of the effects of an observable
\mathsf{F}=\{\mathsf{F}(y):y\in{\mathcal{Y}}\}
caused by
{\mathcal{I}}
, as defined in Eq. (
8
). Then for all
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(43)

where
{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)
denotes the quantum Fisher information of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
in the state
\xi
. Additionally, if
{\mathcal{I}}
is an extremal instrument, then for all
y\in{\mathcal{Y}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(44)

Proof.

Let
\{q_{i},\phi_{i}\}
be an arbitrary ensemble of unit vectors that satisfies
\xi=\sum_{i}q_{i}P_{\phi_{i}}
. We may thus write
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)=\sum_{i}q_{i}\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
, where
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)\equiv\Gamma_{P_{\phi_{i}}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)
. By the conservation law and additivity of
N
, we may therefore rewrite Eq. (
41
) as

[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}},\delta(y)]+\sum_{i}q_{i}\bigg([\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}),\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},N])\bigg),

which, by the sesquilinear mappings
\langle\langle A|B\rangle\rangle_{i}:=\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*}B)-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(A^{*})\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)
, Corollary
A.2
, and Lemma
3.1
gives the bound

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+2\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since both
\mathsf{F}(y)
and
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})
are effects, we have

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\|\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})-\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{F}(y)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\leqslant\frac{1}{2}.

We thus arrive at the bound

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+\left(\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the second line follows from the concavity of the square root. By choosing the ensemble
\{q_{i},\phi_{i}\}
that gives the quantum Fisher information as in Eq. (
13
), we arrive at Eq. (
43
).

Now assume that
{\mathcal{I}}
is an extremal instrument
[
59
]
. This implies that for any pair of instruments
{\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}
and
{\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}
, and any
\lambda\in(0,1)
, the operations of
{\mathcal{I}}
can be decomposed as
{\mathcal{I}}_{x}(\cdot)=\lambda\,{\mathcal{I}}_{x}^{(1)}(\cdot)+(1-\lambda)\,{\mathcal{I}}_{x}^{(2)}(\cdot)
only if
{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}={\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}
. It holds that
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\cdot)
for all
i
, and so we obtain

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\delta(y)\|+2\left(\sum_{i}q_{i}\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i}}\right)\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{F}(y))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Once again, by choosing the ensemble that gives the quantum Fisher information, we arrive at Eq. (
44
).
∎

Appendix I
Proof for Generalised WAY theorem 2

Here, we shall provide a detailed proof for Theorem
3.3
presented in the main text.

Theorem I.1

(Generalised WAY theorem 2)
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. If either
{\mathcal{I}}
is a measurement of the first kind, or the Yanase condition
[\mathsf{Z},{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
is satisfied, then for any effect
\mathsf{E}(x)
that has both eigenvalue 1 and 0, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\mathsf{P}=[\mathsf{E}(x),\mathsf{P}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{P}]=\mathds{O},

(45)

where
\mathsf{P}:=\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)+\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
, with
\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)
and
\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
orthogonal projections onto the eigenvalue-0 and eigenvalue-1 eigenspaces of
\mathsf{E}(x)
, respectively.

Proof.

Let us first note that
\mathsf{E}(x)\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
, and
\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}=\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)
, where
\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}:=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}-\mathsf{E}(x)
. Indeed,
\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)
may equivalently be considered as the projection onto the eigenvalue-1 eigenspace of
\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}
. Now define the operation
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
as
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot):=\mathsf{P}\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)\mathsf{P}
, where
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}
is the unital CP map defined in Eq. (
3
). If
{\mathcal{M}}
is a measurement scheme for
\mathsf{E}
, then
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
. Given
1\geqslant\|\mathsf{Z}(x)\|\geqslant\|\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))\|=\|\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)\|=1
, it follows that
\|\mathsf{Z}(x)\|=1
. Therefore, by the two-positivity of CP maps, and the relation
A^{*}BA\leqslant\|B\|A^{*}A
for self-adjoint
B
, we observe that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)=\|\mathsf{Z}(x)\|\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))\geqslant\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})\geqslant\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x),

and so

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x).

(46)

Now note that by additivity of
N
, and the conservation law, it holds that
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)=\mathsf{P}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{P}+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]\mathsf{P}
. If the Yanase condition holds, we may write

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\mathsf{P}

\displaystyle=[\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)\mathsf{P},\mathsf{P}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{P}]

\displaystyle=[\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)),\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x),N])

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])=\mathds{O}.

The third line follows from Eq. (
46
) and the multiplicability theorem (Corollary
A.1
), and the final line follows from the Yanase condition. As such, we arrive at Eq. (
45
).

Now let us abandon the Yanase condition, but instead assume that
{\mathcal{I}}
is a first-kind measurement for
\mathsf{E}
. This implies that
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
. Since
\|\mathsf{E}(x)\|=1
, then by the two-positivity of CP maps, and the relation
A^{*}BA\leqslant\|B\|A^{*}A
for self-adjoint
B
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)=\|\mathsf{E}(x)\|\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})\geqslant\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})\geqslant\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x),

and so

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x).

(47)

By the same arguments as in item (i) of Proposition
E.1
, one can show from Eq. (
46
) and Eq. (
47
) that
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{n})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{n}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
for all
n\in\mathds{N}
. Consequently, by the same arguments as in item (iv) of Proposition
E.1
, it follows that
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes(\mathsf{Z}(x)-\mathsf{Q}(x)))=\mathds{O}
, where
\mathsf{Q}(x)
is the projection onto the eigenvalue-1 eigenspace of
\mathsf{Z}(x)
, and
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}((\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{P}_{1}(x))\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathds{O}
. Moreover, by Eq. (
46
) , Eq. (
47
), the multiplicability theorem (Corollary
A.1
), and defining
\mathsf{Z}(x)^{\perp}:=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}-\mathsf{Z}(x)
, it follows that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{\perp})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{\perp})=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)=\mathds{O},

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))=\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)^{\perp}\mathsf{P}\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)=\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)=\mathds{O},

and so by the same arguments as in items (v) and (vi) of Proposition
E.1
it follows that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(A\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)A\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})

(48)

for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. By additivity of
N
, and the conservation law, we may therefore write

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\mathsf{P}

\displaystyle=[\mathsf{P}\mathsf{E}(x)\mathsf{P},\mathsf{P}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{P}]

\displaystyle=[\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}),\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)]

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{E}(x)\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},N])

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}([\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathds{O}.

The third line follows from Eq. (
47
) and Corollary
A.1
, while the final line follows from Eq. (
48
). Once again we arrive at Eq. (
45
).

∎

Appendix J
The Weak Yanase condition from conservation laws

Thus far, we have only considered the case where the measurement interaction
{\mathcal{E}}
between system and apparatus conserves an additive quantity
N
. However, pointer objectification will also result in state changes, and it may be the case that the expected value of
N
will change as a result. Now let us provide a generalised prescription of measurement schemes that captures also the state changes due to pointer objectification. Recall that
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
is a measurement scheme for an observable
\mathsf{E}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
if
\mathsf{E}(x)=\Gamma_{\xi}\circ{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))
. Now consider the tuple
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{J}})
, where
{\mathcal{J}}:=\{{\mathcal{J}}_{x}:x\in{\mathcal{X}}\}
is an instrument acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
.
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
is also a measurement scheme for
\mathsf{E}
if
\mathsf{E}(x)=\Gamma_{\xi}\circ{\mathcal{J}}_{x}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})
. It is straightforward to show that this is satisfied if
{\mathcal{J}}
is compatible with the “Heisenberg-evolved” pointer observable

\displaystyle\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x):={\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)),

(49)

that is, if
{\mathcal{J}}_{x}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)
. We say that
\tilde{\mathcal{M}}
obeys a full (average) conservation law if the channel
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathcal{X}}
fully (on average) conserves a quantity
N
. The operations
{\mathcal{J}}_{x}
can be constructed as a sequential application of the channel
{\mathcal{E}}
followed by the operations of some
\mathsf{Z}
-compatible instrument acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, the latter of which provides a physical characterisation of the pointer objectification process. In such a case, a sufficient condition for conservation of
N
by
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathcal{X}}
is the conservation of
N
by both
{\mathcal{E}}
and the
\mathsf{Z}
-channel. But it may be the case that
{\mathcal{E}}
fully conserves
N
while the
\mathsf{Z}
-channel conserves
N
only on average, and vice versa. In such cases, the channel
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathcal{X}}
will conserve
N
only on average.

By Lemma
C.1
, it holds that if
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathcal{X}}
conserves
N
on average, and if either
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
is sharp or if
{\mathcal{J}}_{\mathcal{X}}
also fully conserves
N
, then

\displaystyle[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau},N]=\mathds{O}.

(50)

This commutation relation is known as the weak Yanase condition
[
32
]
. We note that if
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves
N
on average and if either
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
is sharp or if
{\mathcal{E}}
also fully conserves
N
, then the Yanase condition implies the weak Yanase condition. First, let us assume that
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
is sharp. Since
\mathsf{Z}(x)
is an effect then by two-positivity of CP maps we have
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)={\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))\geqslant{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})\geqslant{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}=\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)
, and so we have
{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)^{2})={\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))^{2}
. On the other hand, if
{\mathcal{E}}
fully conserves
N
then
{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(N^{2})={\mathcal{E}}^{*}(N)^{2}=N^{2}
. In either case, by Corollary
A.1
we have

\displaystyle[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x),N]=[{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)),{\mathcal{E}}^{*}(N)]={\mathcal{E}}^{*}([\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x),N])={\mathcal{E}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}]),

and so if
[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
, then
[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x),N]=\mathds{O}
. Moreover, if
{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot)=U(\cdot)U^{*}
is a unitary channel, and
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves
N
, then
[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x),N]=U^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])U
. In such a case the weak Yanase condition is equivalent to the Yanase condition; multiplying both sides of the equality
U^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])U=\mathds{O}
by
U
from the left and by
U^{*}
from the right shows that
[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x),N]=\mathds{O}\iff[\mathsf{Z}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
. However, in general it may be the case that the weak Yanase condition is satisfied but the Yanase condition is violated.

The following proposition shows that if the weak Yanase condition is satisfied, then the measurability part of the WAY theorem will hold. Moreover, we see that there are cases where a large coherence of the conserved quantity in the apparatus is necessary for good measurements even without a full conservation law—for example, if either the interaction channel
{\mathcal{E}}
or the
\mathsf{Z}
-channel conserves
N
only on average, but
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
is sharp, in which case the weak Yanase condition is guaranteed to hold.

Proposition J.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an observable acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and let
\|\epsilon(x)\|
be the error in measuring the effects of the target observable
\mathsf{E}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{M}}
satisfies the weak Yanase condition
[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau},N]=\mathds{O}
, where
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
is the Heisenberg-evolved pointer observable defined in Eq. (
49
) and
N={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. Then for all
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
it holds that

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+2\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\bigg(2\|\epsilon(x)\|+\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(51)

and

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(52)

where
\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right):=\mathrm{tr}[N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}\xi]-\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]^{2}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)
is the the quantum Fisher information of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
in
\xi
as defined in Eq. (
13
). Additionally, if
{\mathcal{M}}
is a measurement scheme for
\mathsf{E}
, then for any effect
\mathsf{E}(x)
that has both eigenvalue 1 and 0, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\mathsf{P}=[\mathsf{E}(x),\mathsf{P}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{P}]=\mathds{O},

(53)

where
\mathsf{P}=\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)+\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
, with
\mathsf{P}_{0}(x)
and
\mathsf{P}_{1}(x)
orthogonal projections onto the eigenvalue-0 and eigenvalue-1 eigenspaces of
\mathsf{E}(x)
, respectively.

Proof.

By Eq. (
7
), we may write
\epsilon(x):=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))-\mathsf{E}(x)\equiv\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x))-\mathsf{E}(x)
. By additivity of
N
we have
\Gamma_{\xi}(N)={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
, and so we may write

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]=[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}},\epsilon(x)]+[\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)),\Gamma_{\xi}(N)].

Since
\Gamma_{\xi}
is a channel, and the weak Yanase condition
[\mathsf{Z}^{\tau},N]=\mathds{O}
holds, then by the sesquilinear map
\langle\langle A|B\rangle\rangle:=\Gamma_{\xi}(A^{*}B)-\Gamma_{\xi}(A^{*})\Gamma_{\xi}(B)
and Corollary
A.2
we obtain

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\|\leqslant 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\|\epsilon(x)\|+2\|\Gamma_{\xi}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}(N)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x))^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}.

As shown in Lemma
3.1
, additivity of
N
implies that
\|\Gamma_{\xi}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}(N)^{2}\|=\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right)
. On the other hand, by Lemma
A.2
we obtain
\|\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x)^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}(\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}(x))^{2}\|\leqslant 2\|\epsilon(x)\|+\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|
. As such, we obtain the bound in Eq. (
51
). Eq. (
52
) and Eq. (
53
) are trivially obtained by adapting the arguments in Proposition
G.1
and Theorem
I.1
to the above, i.e., by replacing
\mathsf{Z}
with
\mathsf{Z}^{\tau}
, and replacing the operations
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}^{\mathcal{E}}
and
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}^{\mathcal{E}}
with the operations
\Gamma_{\phi_{i}}
and
\Gamma_{\xi,\mathsf{P}}
, respectively.
∎

Appendix K
Proof of the “converse” WAY theorem

Here, we provide a proof for Proposition
4.1
presented in the main text.

Proposition K.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
fully conserves an additive quantity
N={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
, and that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}
fully conserves
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Define the eigenspace of
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
that is involved during the measurement process as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}(\text{meas}):=\bigcup_{\rho\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})}\supp(\Lambda(\rho))\cup\supp(\xi)\subseteq{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},

where
\Lambda
is the conjugate channel to
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}
defined in Eq. (
5
). Then either
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}(\text{meas})
is contained within a single degenerate eigenspace of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, or
\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right):=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}}\xi]-\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]^{2}
must be large. Additionally, if
{\mathcal{I}}
is an extremal instrument, and if
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}(\text{meas})
is not contained within a single degenerate eigenspace of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, then the quantum Fisher information
{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)
as defined in Eq. (
13
) must be large.

Proof.

Let us first observe that by Eq. (
10
), if
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves
N
on average, then
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
implies that irrespective of the apparatus preparation
\xi
, it holds that
\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\Lambda(\rho)]=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]
for all
\rho\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
or, equivalently, that
\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. That is, the expected value of the apparatus part of the conserved quantity does not change as a result of the measurement interaction. While average conservation does not imply that the variance must also stay the same, this implication can be shown to follow in the case of full conservation. By Definition
2
, full conservation of
N
by
{\mathcal{E}}
implies that
\Gamma_{\xi}(N^{2})=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})
, where
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}
is the channel defined in Eq. (
3
). Given that
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\cdot\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\cdot)
and
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\cdot)=\Lambda^{*}(\cdot)
, we thus obtain

\displaystyle{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}+2\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})+2\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}))+\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})+2\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})+\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}})

\displaystyle={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}})+2{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})+\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}})

\displaystyle={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}+2\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}}).

In the second line, we have used the fact that full conservation of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
by
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}
implies that
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})^{2}={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}^{2}}
, and the multiplicability theorem (Corollary
A.1
). It follows that
\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{k}})=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{k}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
for
k=1,2
. As such, for any input state
\rho
of the system to be measured, it holds that

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\Lambda(\rho)}\right)=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}}\Lambda(\rho)]-\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\Lambda(\rho)]^{2}=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{2}}\xi]-\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]^{2}=\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right).

Now assume that
\xi
is an eigenstate of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, i.e., that there exists
c\in\mathds{R}
such that
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi=c\,\xi
. Since such a condition is equivalent to a vanishing variance, then we see that
\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\Lambda(\rho)}\right)=\mathrm{Var}\left({{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi}\right)=0
, and so
\Lambda(\rho)
must also be eigenstates of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
with the same eigenvalue
c
. In fact, in such a case it holds that
\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{k}})=\Lambda^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})^{k}=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]^{k}\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
for
k=1,2
, which implies that
\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{k}}\Lambda(\rho)]=\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}^{k}}\xi]=c^{k}
for all
k\in\mathds{N}
and
\rho\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. In other words, if
\xi
is an eigenstate of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, then
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
must be “effectively” fully degenerate, i.e.,
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}(\text{meas})
must be contained within a single degenerate eigenspace of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
. Therefore, if
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}(\text{meas})
contains more than one degenerate eigenspace of
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, the apparatus must be prepared in a state with a large uncertainty in
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
.

Now assume that
{\mathcal{I}}
is an extremal instrument, i.e., that for any
\lambda\in(0,1)
, the operations of
{\mathcal{I}}
admit a decomposition
{\mathcal{I}}_{x}(\cdot)=\lambda\,{\mathcal{I}}_{x}^{(1)}(\cdot)+(1-\lambda)\,{\mathcal{I}}_{x}^{(2)}(\cdot)
only if
{\mathcal{I}}={\mathcal{I}}^{(1)}={\mathcal{I}}^{(2)}
. In such a case, it follows that if
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
is a measurement scheme for
{\mathcal{I}}
, then for any pure state decomposition
\xi=\sum_{i}q_{i}P_{\phi_{i}}
, it holds that
({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\phi_{i},{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
must also be measurement schemes for
{\mathcal{I}}
. By the above arguments, it follows that unless
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
is effectively degenerate, then each
\phi_{i}
must have a large uncertainty in
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
, i.e., the quantum Fisher information
{\mathcal{Q}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi)
must be large.
∎

Appendix L
Faithful fixed states and measurement disturbance

Recall from Proposition
D.1
and Theorem
H.1
that under a conservation law, an
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
will no disturb an observable
\mathsf{F}
only if

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{E}(x),\mathsf{F}(y)]\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|\mathsf{E}(x)-\mathsf{E}(x)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\forall\,x\in{\mathcal{X}},\,y\in{\mathcal{Y}},

and

\displaystyle\|[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])\|

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\|\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N^{2})-\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\|{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathsf{F}(y)^{2})-\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}\qquad\forall\,y\in{\mathcal{Y}}.

If
\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies\mathsf{F}^{2}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
, then the upper bound of the above equations vanish, and so
\mathsf{F}
will be non-disturbed only if
[\mathsf{F},\mathsf{E}]=\mathds{O}
and
[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
. By the multiplicability theorem (Corollary
A.1
), the second condition can be shown to simplify to
[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[\mathsf{F}(y),{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})]
, which reads
[\mathsf{F}(y),{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})-{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
. In Appendix (
D
) we saw that the implication
\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies\mathsf{F}^{2}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
holds if either
\mathsf{F}
is sharp, rank-1, or a coarse-graining of a sharp observable. We now show that if
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}})
is a von Neumann algebra, so that
\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies\mathsf{F}^{2}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
, similar and stronger constraints will hold for all observables. To this end, let us first prove a useful lemma.

Lemma L.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
, and that
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra. Then for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, the following implication holds:
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies[A,N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies[A,N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}]\in\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Recall that for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, we have
{\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}(A)=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})
, where
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}
is the channel defined in Eq. (
3
). Average conservation of
N
by
{\mathcal{E}}
implies that

\displaystyle{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})+\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}).

Since
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra, it follows that for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
,
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})\implies A^{*}A,AA^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
which, by Corollary
A.1
, implies that
A\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}((A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})B)
and
\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)A=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(B(A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}))
for all
B\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. Therefore, for all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
we have

\displaystyle[A,{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]

\displaystyle=[A,\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})]+[A,\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})]

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}])+\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}([A,{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})={\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}}([A,{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]).

Consequently, we see that
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})\implies[A,{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
. But as shown in Lemma
C.1
, if
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra then
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\subset\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. It follows that
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})\implies[A,{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\in\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
.

∎

We are now ready to prove the following:

Theorem L.1
.

Let
\mathsf{E}:=\{\mathsf{E}(x):x\in{\mathcal{X}}\}
and
\mathsf{F}:=\{\mathsf{F}(y):y\in{\mathcal{Y}}\}
be observables acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
, and assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}
on average, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. If
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra, then the following hold:

(i)

\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
only if
\mathsf{F}
commutes with
\mathsf{E}
, with
\Delta{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}:={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})-{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and with
\{[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]:x\in{\mathcal{X}}\}
.

(ii)

{\mathcal{I}}
is a measurement of the first kind only if
\mathsf{E}
is a commutative observable that commutes with
N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
.

(iii)

{\mathcal{I}}
is repeatable only if
\mathsf{E}
is sharp and commutes with
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
.

Proof.

(i):

By Lemma
L.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\subset\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. Therefore,
\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies[\mathsf{E},\mathsf{F}]=\mathds{O}
. Moreover, the conservation law implies that
[\mathsf{F},{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
must hold. Since
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
is an algebra, then
\mathsf{F}(y)\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})\implies\mathsf{F}(y)^{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
. By Corollary
A.1
, it follows that
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{F}(y){N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})=\mathsf{F}(y){\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\mathsf{F}(y))={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\mathsf{F}(y)
, and so
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[\mathsf{F}(y),{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})]
. As such,
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}([\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}])=[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\implies[\mathsf{F}(y),{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}({N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})-{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
. Moreover, by Lemma
L.1

\mathsf{F}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
implies
[\mathsf{F}(y),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\in\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. Since
[A,[B,C]]=[B,[A,C]]+[C,[B,A]]
holds for all
A,B,C\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, while
[\mathsf{E},\mathsf{F}]=\mathds{O}
, then this implies that
[\mathsf{F}(y),[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]]=\mathds{O}
.

(ii):

{\mathcal{I}}
is a measurement of the first kind only if
\mathsf{E}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
. Commutativity of
\mathsf{E}
follows from (i). Now, let us define
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t):=e^{\mathfrak{i}t\mathsf{E}(x)}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}e^{-\mathfrak{i}t\mathsf{E}(x)}
. We may write

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t)=\mathfrak{i}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t)],

from which we obtain

\displaystyle{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t)

\displaystyle={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathfrak{i}\int^{t}_{0}dt_{1}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t_{1})]

\displaystyle={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathfrak{i}\int^{t}_{0}dt_{1}[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]-\int^{t}_{0}dt_{1}\int^{t_{1}}_{0}dt_{2}\,e^{\mathfrak{i}t_{2}\mathsf{E}(x)}[\mathsf{E}(x),[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]]e^{-\mathfrak{i}t_{2}\mathsf{E}(x)}

\displaystyle={N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}+\mathfrak{i}t[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}].

In the final line we have used (i), which implies that
[\mathsf{E}(x),[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]]=\mathds{O}
holds for all
x
. We thus obtain the inequality

\displaystyle 2\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|\geqslant\|{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}(t)-{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\|=|t|\|[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]\|

for all
t
. Given that
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
is a bounded operator, this is clearly satisfied only if
[\mathsf{E}(x),{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}]=\mathds{O}
.

(iii):

Since repeatability implies first-kindness, then by (ii)
\mathsf{E}
must commute with
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Sharpness of
\mathsf{E}
follows from item (i) of Proposition
E.1
which gives
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\mathsf{E}(x)^{2})=\mathsf{E}(x)
for a repeatable
{\mathcal{I}}
, and the fact that if
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra then
\mathsf{E}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
implies that
\mathsf{E}^{2}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
.

∎

We shall now give two examples where
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra, and so the implications of Theorem
L.1
hold.

Lemma L.2
.

Consider the Lüders
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}^{L}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, defined in Eq. (
1
). If either (i)
\dim({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})<\infty
, or (ii)
\mathsf{E}
is commutative, then
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof.

Let us first consider (i). Define the complete mixture
\omega:=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}/\dim({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, which is faithful. It follows trivially that
{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}(\omega)=\omega
, and so
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}})
contains a faithful state
\omega
. By Lemma
B.1

{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra. Now let us consider (ii). Recall that
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra if
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})=\{K_{i},K_{i}^{*}\}^{\prime}
, with
\{K_{i}\}
any Kraus representation of
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}

[
55
]
. But for a Lüders instrument, we have
\{K_{i},K_{i}^{*}\}^{\prime}=\{\sqrt{\mathsf{E}(x)}\}^{\prime}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. While
\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
always holds, it was observed that in infinite-dimensional systems there exists
\mathsf{E}
for which
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})\not\subset\mathsf{E}^{\prime}

[
78
,
79
]
. However, it was shown in
[
63
]
that for binary observables, it always holds that
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
. Since binary observables are commutative, this led to the conjecture that the fixed-point set of the Lüders
\mathsf{E}
-channel is the commutant of
\mathsf{E}
for all commutative observables
[
78
]
, making
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
a von Neumann algebra, which was later proven to be the case
[
76
,
77
]
.

∎

Let us highlight an interesting consequence of the above lemma:

Corollary L.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an
\mathsf{E}
-compatible Lüders instrument
{\mathcal{I}}^{L}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. Assume that
\mathsf{E}
is commutative, and that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average. It holds that
\mathsf{E}
commutes with
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
.

Proof.

If
\mathsf{E}\subset\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}
is a von Neumann algebra. Moreover, it holds that
\mathsf{E}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({{\mathcal{I}}^{L}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{*})
, so that the Lüders instrument for a commutative observable is a measurement of the first kind. It follows from item (ii) of Theorem
L.1
that
\mathsf{E}
must commute with
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
.
∎

Lemma L.3
.

Assume that
\dim({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})<\infty
, and let
\mathsf{G}:=\{\mathsf{G}(z):z\in{\mathcal{Z}}\}
be a rank-1 observable acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
. If an instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
does not disturb
\mathsf{G}
, then
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra.

Proof.

Since
\mathsf{G}(z)
are rank-
1
effects, we may write
\mathsf{G}(z)=\lambda_{z}P_{z}
, with
\lambda_{z}\in(0,1]
and
P_{z}
a rank-1 projection. It follows that
\mathsf{G}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})\implies\{P_{z}\}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
. But we may write

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}[P_{z}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}(P_{z})]=\mathrm{tr}[{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(P_{z})P_{z}]=\mathrm{tr}[P_{z}P_{z}]=\mathrm{tr}[P_{z}]=1,

and so
\mathsf{G}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})\implies\{P_{z}\}\subset{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}})
. Consequently, we may construct the faithful state
\omega=\sum_{z}p_{z}P_{z}
with
p_{z}>0
and
\sum_{z}p_{z}=1
, so that
\omega\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}})
. By Lemma
B.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})
is a von Neumann algebra.
∎

Appendix M
Non-faithful fixed states and measurement disturbance

In this section we analyse the structure of the fixed-point set of arbitrary channels, which need not contain a faithful state. From here, the results of the previous section are generalised. We then provide novel quantitative bounds for first-kind measurements which complement our generalisation of the WAY theorem given in Theorem
3.3
.

Due to the Schauder–Tychonoff fixed point theorem
[
82
]
, all channels
\Phi:{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
have at least one fixed state. However, it may be that none of these are faithful. In such a case, the fixed-point set of the dual channel is not necessarily a von Neumann algebra, but rather forms an operator space
[
83
]
. This setting has been much less investigated, and its analysis
forms the first part of this section. While the discussion thus far has been applicable for infinite-dimensional systems—except in some examples—in this section we shall always assume that
d:=\dim({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})<\infty
.

M.1
Fixed-point structure of arbitrary channels

Consider a channel
\Phi:{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, and its dual in the Heisenberg picture
\Phi^{*}:{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. We may define the channels

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}(\cdot):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Phi^{n}(\cdot),

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(\cdot):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Phi^{*n}(\cdot),

(54)

where
\Phi^{n}
denotes
n
consecutive applications of
\Phi
. Note that these limits exists since
d<\infty
. According to the Jordan decomposition theorem,
\Phi^{*}
is represented as a summation of projections onto eigenspaces multiplied by the corresponding eigenvalues, and nilpotent operators whose eigenspaces are invariant subspaces;
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}
corresponds to the projection onto the subspace
with eigenvalue
1
. The fixed-point set
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
forms an operator space, i.e., a norm-closed vector subspace of the codomain of
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
, and
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}
is a CP projection onto
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
.

Lemma M.1
.

Consider the channels
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}
and
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}
defined in Eq. (
54
). These have the following properties:

(i)

\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi^{*}=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}
and
\Phi\circ\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}
.

(ii)

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}({\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
and
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}({\mathcal{T}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)
.

Proof.

(i) is trivial, and so we shall only prove (ii). Let us first consider the Heisenberg picture channel
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}
. That
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})\subset{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})
is trivial. Conversely, for any
A\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})
, by (i) we have
\Phi^{*}(A)=\Phi^{*}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=A
, and therefore
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})\subset{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
. It follows that
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
. Similarly, for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
it holds that
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)
, and thus
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}({\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))\subset{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})
. That
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})\subset\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}({\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))
is trivial, and so we also have
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}({\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})
. The relations in (ii) for the Schrödinger picture channel
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}
follow from similar arguments.
∎

Now consider the state

\displaystyle\rho_{0}:=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}\left(\frac{1}{d}\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\right).

(55)

By Lemma
M.1
, it holds that
\rho_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)
. We define by
P
the minimal support projection on
\rho_{0}
:

\displaystyle P:=\min\{Q:Q\text{ is a projection, }\rho_{0}=Q\rho_{0}Q\}.

(56)

In other words, for all projections
Q
such that
\rho_{0}=Q\rho_{0}Q
, it holds that
Q\geqslant P
. Note that if
P=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
then
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)
contains a faithful state. The following lemma provides some useful properties of
P
.

Lemma M.2
.

Consider the state
\rho_{0}
defined in Eq. (
55
), with the minimal support projection
P
as defined in Eq. (
56
), and
P^{\perp}:=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}-P
its orthogonal complement. The following hold:

(i)

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(P)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
and
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(P^{\perp})=\mathds{O}
.

(ii)

For all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
,
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(PAP)
.

(iii)

P=\min\{Q:Q\mbox{ is a projection, }\rho=Q\rho Q\,\ \forall\,\rho\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)\}
.

(iv)

P=\min\{Q:Q\mbox{ is a projection, }\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(Q)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
}
.

(v)

\Phi^{*}(P)\geqslant P
and
\Phi^{*}(P^{\perp})\leqslant P^{\perp}
.

Proof.

(i):

Since
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}
is a channel, and
\mathds{O}<P\leqslant\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
, it follows that
\mathds{O}\leqslant\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(P)\leqslant\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. But by Eq. (
55
)
\mathrm{tr}[\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(P)]=d\,\mathrm{tr}[\rho_{0}P]=d
, and so
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(P)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. It trivially follows that
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(P^{\perp})=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}})-\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(P)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}-\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}=\mathds{O}
.

(ii):

Since
P
is positive, then by (i) and Lemma
A.3
it holds that
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(P^{\perp}A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(AP^{\perp})=\mathds{O}
for all
A
. The claim follows by noting that we may write
A=(P+P^{\perp})A(P+P^{\perp})
.

(iii):

By Lemma
M.1
and (ii), for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
\rho\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)
we have
\mathrm{tr}[A\rho]=\mathrm{tr}[\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(A)\rho]=\mathrm{tr}[\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(PAP)\rho]=\mathrm{tr}[AP\rho P]
, and so
\rho\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)\implies\rho=P\rho P
. Since
P
is the minimal support projection on
\rho_{0}\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi)
, the claim follows.

(iv):

By (i),
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(P)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
holds. Suppose another projection
Q
satisfies
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(Q)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. Then (ii) implies that
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(PQP)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(Q)=\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. As we have
1=\mathrm{tr}[(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}/d)\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(PQP)]=\mathrm{tr}[\rho_{0}PQP]=\mathrm{tr}[\rho_{0}Q]
, it follows that
Q\geqslant P
.

(v):

Since
P
is the smallest projection satisfying
\rho_{0}=P\rho_{0}P
, while
\mathrm{tr}[\rho_{0}\Phi^{*}(P)]=\mathrm{tr}[\Phi(\rho_{0})P]=\mathrm{tr}[\rho_{0}P]=1
, it follows that
\Phi^{*}(P)\geqslant P
, and hence
\Phi^{*}(P^{\perp})=\Phi^{*}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}})-\Phi^{*}(P)\leqslant\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}-P=P^{\perp}
.

∎

Let us now define the operations

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(\cdot):=P\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(\cdot)P,

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{P}(\cdot):=P\Phi^{*}(\cdot)P.

(57)

Note that
\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}:{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
is not necessarily unital, since

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}})=P\leqslant\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}
. However, the restriction of
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}
to
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
,
which is also denoted by
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}
, is unital and hence a channel, since
P
is the identity in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(P)=P
. The same holds for
\Phi_{P}^{*}
.

Lemma M.3
.

Consider the operations defined in Eq. (
57
). The following hold:

(i)

\Phi^{*}_{P}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{P}(PAP)
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
.

(ii)

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(PAP)
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
.

(iii)

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}
is a completely positive projection

{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
.

(iv)

{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}({\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))
.

(v)

\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}
is a bijection from

{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})
to
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
.

(vi)

The inverse of
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}:{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})\to{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
is
\mathrm{Ad}_{P}:A\mapsto PAP
.

Proof.

(i):

By item (v) of Lemma
M.2
, it holds that
\mathds{O}\leqslant\Phi^{*}_{P}(P^{\perp})=P\Phi^{*}(P^{\perp})P\leqslant PP^{\perp}P=\mathds{O}
, and so
\Phi^{*}_{P}(P^{\perp})=\mathds{O}
. By Lemma
A.3
, it holds that
\Phi_{P}^{*}(P^{\perp}A)=\Phi_{P}^{*}(AP^{\perp})=\mathds{O}
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. Since
A=(P+P^{\perp})A(P+P^{\perp})
, the claim follows.

(ii):

By item (ii) of Lemma
M.2
,
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(A)=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(PAP)
. The claim immediately follows.

(iii):

By item (i) of Lemma
M.1
and (ii) above, we have

\displaystyle\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(P\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)P)=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=P\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}\circ\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)P=P\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)P=\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(A).

(iv):

By (iii), it follows that for any
A\in{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
,
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(A)\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})
and so
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}({\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}))\subset{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})
. The converse is trivial.

(v):

For all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})
, there exists an operator
PAP\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*})
such that
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(PAP)=\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(A)=A
. Therefore,
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}
is surjective. Now assume that there exists
A\in{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*})
such that
\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av}}(A)=\mathds{O}
. This implies that
A=\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}(A)=P\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(A)P=\mathds{O}
. Therefore,
\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}
is injective.

(vi):

Follows from above.

∎

The above results have the following useful consequence:

Proposition M.1
.

Consider the operations
\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}
and
\Phi^{*}_{P}
defined in Eq. (
57
).
It holds that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{P})=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*})P=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})P

is a von Neumann algebra in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
.

Proof.

Recall that the operation
\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}:{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
is unital, where the unit in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
is
P
. Moreover,
\rho_{0}
as defined in Eq. (
55
) is a faithful fixed point of
\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}
in
{\mathcal{T}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. By Lemma
B.1
, the fixed-point set
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})
is a von Neumann algebra in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. Now we need only show that
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{P})=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*})P=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})P
. That
P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*})P=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})P
trivially follows from Lemma
M.1
, which gives
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*})
. That
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})=P{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})P
follows from item (vi) of Lemma
M.3
, since the map
\mathrm{Ad}_{P}:A\mapsto PAP
is a bijection from
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*})
to
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})
. To show that
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{P})
, let us first define the operation
\Phi_{P,\mathrm{av}}^{*}:{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
as

\displaystyle\Phi_{P,\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\Phi_{P}^{*n}(\cdot).

But by item (i) of Lemma
M.3
,
\Phi^{*}_{P}(A)=\Phi^{*}_{P}(PAP)
, and so
\Phi_{P}^{*n}(A)=P\Phi^{*n}(A)P
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
n\in\mathds{N}
. It follows that

\displaystyle\Phi_{P,\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}P\Phi^{*n}(\cdot)P=P\Phi_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\cdot)P=:\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P}(\cdot).

Since
\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}=\Phi_{P,\mathrm{av}}^{*}
, it trivially follows that
\Phi_{P}^{*}\circ\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}=\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}\circ\Phi_{P}^{*}=\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}\circ\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}=\Phi_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}
. Therefore,
{\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{\mathrm{av},P})={\mathcal{F}}(\Phi^{*}_{P})
can be shown by the same arguments as in Lemma
M.1

∎

M.2
Measurement disturbance revisited

We are now ready to address the question of measurement disturbance, generalising the observations of Theorem
L.1
. As before, let
{\mathcal{I}}:=\{{\mathcal{I}}_{x}:x\in{\mathcal{X}}\}
be an
\mathsf{E}
-compatible instrument, with
{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}(\cdot):=\sum_{x}{\mathcal{I}}_{x}(\cdot)
the corresponding
\mathsf{E}
-channel. By Eq. (
54
) and Eq. (
57
) we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\cdot)

\displaystyle:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*n}(\cdot),\qquad{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*}(\cdot):=P{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathrm{av}}^{*}(\cdot)P,\qquad{\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*}(\cdot):=P{\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*}(\cdot)P,

where as in Eq. (
56
),
P
is the minimal support projection of
\rho_{0}:={\mathcal{I}}_{\mathrm{av}}(\frac{1}{d}\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}})
, which corresponds with the minimal projection on the support of
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}})
. By Proposition
M.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathrm{av},P}^{*})={\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})=P{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathrm{av}})P=P{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{\mathcal{X}})P
is a von Neumann algebra in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
. We define by
P\mathsf{E}P:=\{P\mathsf{E}(x)P:x\in{\mathcal{X}}\}
the restriction of
\mathsf{E}
to an observable in
P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, which satisfies
\sum_{x}P\mathsf{E}(x)P=P
, and
(P\mathsf{E}P)^{\prime}:=\{A\in{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}):[P\mathsf{E}(x)P,A]=\mathds{O}\,\forall x\}
denotes the commutant of
P\mathsf{E}P
in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
.
P\mathsf{F}P
is similarly defined.

Before generalising Theorem
L.1
for the case where
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}})
may not contain any faithful states, and thus
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}}^{*})
may not necessarily be a von Neumann algebra, let us first prove a generalisation of Lemma
L.1
.

Lemma M.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}:=({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}},\xi,{\mathcal{E}},\mathsf{Z})
be a measurement scheme for an
\mathsf{E}
-instrument
{\mathcal{I}}
acting in
{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}
, and let
P
be the minimal support projection on
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{\mathcal{X}})
. It holds that
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})\subset(P\mathsf{E}P)^{\prime}
. Additionally, if
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average, where
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
and
{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{s}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
, then for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}(\mathsf{P}{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
the following implication holds:
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})\implies[A,PN_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}P]\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})\implies[A,PN_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}P]\in(P\mathsf{E}P)^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By Eq. (
3
), let us define the operation
\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}:{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}),B\mapsto P\Gamma_{\xi}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)P
. It is easily verified that
{\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*}(A)=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})
for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, and
P\mathsf{E}(x)P=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))
. Note that by the same arguments as item (i) of Lemma
M.3
, it can easily be shown that
\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}((P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})B(P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}))
holds for all
B\in{\mathcal{L}}({\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
. It follows that
\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}
is unital when restricted to
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})\to{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, and we may equivalently write
P\mathsf{E}(x)P=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))
.

By Proposition
M.1
,
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
is a von Neumann algebra in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, and so for all
A\in{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, if
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
, then
A^{*}A,AA^{*}\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
. By Corollary
A.1
it holds that for all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
and
B\in{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}\otimes{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})
we have
A\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}((A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})B)
and
\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(B)A=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(B(A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}))
. It follows that for all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
and
x\in{\mathcal{X}}
we have

\displaystyle[A,P\mathsf{E}(x)P]=[A,\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x))]=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}([A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},P\otimes\mathsf{Z}(x)])=\mathds{O},

and so
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{P})\subset(P\mathsf{E}P)^{\prime}
.

Now let us assume that
{\mathcal{E}}
conserves an additive quantity
N=N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}+\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}\otimes N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}
on average. This implies that
P\Gamma_{\xi}(N)P=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(N)=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}((P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})N(P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}))
, and so

\displaystyle P{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}P+\mathrm{tr}[{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}\xi]P=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})+\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}).

Since
{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}^{*}_{P})
is a von Neumann algebra in
{\mathcal{L}}(P{\mathcal{H}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}})
, then by Corollary
A.1
and the arguments above, it follows that for all
A\in{\mathcal{F}}({\mathcal{I}}_{P}^{*})
we have

\displaystyle[A,P{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}P]

\displaystyle=[A,\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}})]+[A,\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}(P\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}})]

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}([A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},P{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{S}}}}}P\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}])+\Gamma_{\xi,P}^{\mathcal{E}}([A\otimes\mathds{1}_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}},P\otimes{N_{\!{\,{\mathcal{A}}}}}])
